English
  • English

An animated, cited guide for students, people who have had a stroke, and their families and carers. Educational only, not medical advice. Your place is saved on this device.

Not medical adviceThe Stroke Companion is an educational synthesis of published literature for study and understanding. If you think someone is having a stroke, call your local emergency number. It does not replace a doctor, nurse, physiotherapist, occupational therapist, speech and language therapist or the rest of a stroke team. Scores such as the NIHSS, ICH score, ABCD2, CHA2DS2-VASc and ASPECTS are teaching aids, never for an individual's diagnosis, prognosis or treatment. Doses, thresholds and guideline statements are quoted as published, never as instructions. Outcomes are population statistics; they are not predictions for any person.

Part 6 · Context & review

Sources

Every reference behind the Stroke Companion: randomised trials, meta-analyses, registries, reviews, AHA/ASA, ESO, NICE and other guidance, US drug labels and trial registrations. The small numbers throughout the app point here; filter by research area or search.

Research passes ran in parallel, one per area: epidemiology and history; blood supply, the ischaemic cascade, causes and TIA; haemorrhagic stroke; recognition, imaging and the NIHSS; thrombolysis and thrombectomy; stroke unit and nursing care; secondary prevention; rehabilitation and life after stroke; and the research frontier. Before inclusion each source was checked, where possible, against PubMed, Europe PMC, Crossref, ClinicalTrials.gov or the regulator's label, and the green tag on an entry names the service that confirmed it. Works found by more than one pass appear once, with any extra keys listed beside them.

Several guideline full texts could not be read, including parts of the 2026 AHA/ASA early management guideline. Where that matters, modules say the statement is reported through a secondary source. Entries marked not matched in a research database are pages with no PMID or DOI, or works that could not be confirmed.

Stroke Companion is an educational synthesis of the published literature on stroke, made for medical, nursing and therapy courses and for people who have had a stroke, their families and carers.

It is not medical advice. It cannot diagnose. If you think someone is having a stroke, call your local emergency number. Nothing here should start, stop or change a medicine, a therapy or a care plan without your own doctor or stroke team. The NIHSS, ICH score, ABCD2, CHA2DS2-VASc, ASPECTS and other scales are for learning, never for an individual's diagnosis, prognosis or treatment. Doses, thresholds and guideline statements are quoted as published by the AHA/ASA, ESO, NICE and drug labels, never as instructions, and outcome figures are population statistics, not predictions for any person. Please check the original sources, which are listed in full in the Sources module.

Part of Medical Companions, animated and cited guides to one condition each. Also there: the Heart Attack Companion, on arterial disease that strokes and heart attacks share, the Alzheimer's Companion, on the dementia that vascular cognitive impairment can overlap with, and the Sepsis Companion, on the critical care that some people with severe stroke also need.

Edited by Moshe Malka, who is not a clinician. These guides are written with AI from published sources and have not yet been reviewed by a clinician. How they are made · Privacy

Questions, corrections or ideas: hello@medcompanions.org
